ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e2416	Please, Mr. Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e2431	Ladies and gentlemen! According to the applicable legal situation, each individual application for the export of war material is subject to the approval criteria laid down in Section 3 para. 1 of the Federal Law on the Entry, Export and transit of War Material, and the decision on an authorisation will then be made in each individual case. I do not think that there is a need for a regulation to allow for specific applications for export. However, the question arises to what extent the restrictions of war material law could be detrimental to the overall interests of the Republic of Austria and whether differentiation between offensive and defensive weapons or spare parts supplies would be beneficial. Moreover, in our law we have also given no distinction between the treatment of the aggressors and the victims. The Federal Chancellor's Office's Constitutional Service will once again carry out a comparative review of war material legislation and the practice of comparable European states. This has happened before. It is too early to draw definitive legal conclusions on this matter.

This Convention is also in line with the position long represented by Austria that the cooperation of the authorities in the field of home and justice is an important prerequisite for combating especially organised crime. The Council invited Member States to ratify the Convention and the Protocol as quickly as possible. In the field of the so-called third pillar, the European Council also approved the principle of establishing a European Monitoring Centre on Racism and Xenophobia. We have also submitted an application for Austria, for Vienna in this respect. In the field of external relations, an agreement has finally been reached with Slovenia that Slovenia will be included in the strategies for the preparation of the accession of the Central and Eastern European countries. It was also decided that the first phase of negotiations with the Central and Eastern European States should coincide with the start of negotiations with Cyprus and Malta. Both decisions were clearly supported by Austria. On the issue of the Intergovernmental Conference, the European Council asked that the Irish Presidency prepare a general framework for a draft revision of the Treaties for the next Dublin summit. This should include, in particular, the objectives of close proximity to citizens, issues of foreign and security policy and institutional reform.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e3865	Mr. Federal Chancellor! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00014/index.shtml -- 14/M]] Do you still hold Saudi Arabia, which proves "Amnesty international" numerous human rights violations every year, for a suitable target country of Austrian arms exports?
ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e3893	Please, Mr. Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e3908	Ladies and gentlemen! Although under the ECOSOC Resolution 1503/1958 against Saudi Arabia a procedure is pending before the UN Commission on Human Rights for serious and systematic human rights violations, it cannot in principle be assumed that any export of war material to Saudi Arabia would be incompatible with the current legal situation. After all, § 3 para. 1 Z 3 of the Federal Law on the Import, Export and transit of War Material is opposed to the authorisation of an application for the export of war material if, due to severe and repeated human rights violations in the recipient country, there is a risk that the supplied war material will be used to suppress human rights. For example, whether this danger exists depends on the type of material. Therefore, the decision to issue an authorisation will have to be taken in each individual case.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e4554	Federal Chancellor! On 21 and 22. The OECD Council of Ministers took place in May last month, under the Austrian Presidency. My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00012/index.shtml -- 12/M]] What are the main results of the OECD Council of Ministers, which took place on 21 and 22. It was held by the Austrian Presidency on 12 May 1996 and held under the chairmanship of the Austrian Presidency.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e4586	Mr. Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e4601	Ladies and gentlemen! The Council of the Organization for Economic Cooperation and Development is the highest body of this organisation and meets once a year at ministerial level. For the first time since 1979, Austria has been given the presidency, and I have exercised it together with the Federal Minister for Finance. The main topics were growth and employment and the strengthening of the multilateral system and the future of the OECD. The issues of growth and employment have been covered by an action plan, the main elements of which include the continuation of stability-oriented fiscal and monetary policy, the acceleration of structural reforms, including deregulation measures, and active measures to combat unemployment, primarily by increasing the quality of training for workers. The second important result was a rejection of any kind of unilateralism, of any kind of protectionism and so on and a commitment to the open world trade economy, so that a clear commitment to respect for world trade rules and an extension of this organisation as a priority forum for trade liberalisation. There were new topics. I stress the link between trade and minimum labour standards in order to combat social dumping, wage dumping and labour cost dumping in this way. The aim was to ensure that these minimum labour standards do not apply only in the OECD countries. We should also start negotiations with other countries to ensure that these standards are enforced worldwide. This is, of course, a very ambitious task. Then there was the debate on the future of the OECD. In particular, it has been defined by the European side as an indispensable role to maintain OECD as a forum for economic cooperation between the high-level pensioners and the industrialised countries. From actuality, I add: The OECD has also repeatedly been called upon to provide the basic economic reports for the so-called G-7 meetings. This is important because in this way those countries that do not participate in the G-7 summit can also bring their opinions into the foundations of the G7 consultations. That is to say, ladies and gentlemen, we have not only devoted ourselves to standard issues such as job security and growth, but also clearly recognized that new problems are to be addressed with new partners in the world-wide networked economy.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e5380	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Doctors between megatechnology and magic – this is the title of the latest "spiegel" special edition. This revitalises an interesting discussion in the public that deals with the gods in white, which are once praised by the media and the other time threatens to be miscredited as black sheep. Whether it's an artificial emergency room in a series-special, whether it's a reality view of the suffering of little Olivia Pilhar or whether it's Mr. Wolf's spade story: Doctors have high season again and are in public discussion. As important changes to the doctor's law are under discussion today – I am pleased to say God, honourable Member – I would like to make some fundamental considerations about the profession of the doctor. [[to Abg. May. Guggenberger]] I do not think anyone will say that a doctor will end his training for purely image-like or purely financial reasons. I think the most important thing about a medical professional must be that he is a philanthropist. Like a good politician, he must love people. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Dr. Leiner.]] Thanks a lot. And any suffering he is faced with is partly also a suffering of the treating doctor. Even if it sounds a little kitschy: Part of what makes it possible for a doctor to practice his profession is to see again and again how a sick child manages his illness. – This is very relative in life – no matter where the level of professional training you are, whether you are a primary doctor or a student. Before I go into further criticisms about the medical law, I would like to draw attention to the framework conditions under which the doctors work today – but this is very important to me. I'll tell you: It wants to go to the hospitals. It really wants it! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] There is still no regulation on the law on working time. There are still doctors who work up to 90 hours a week. There are still 15 night services per month. There are still weekend services that last over 56 hours. I myself have been in the service of a practical doctor from Friday in the morning to Monday in the afternoon – there is still that. You know that at 23. This year, the EU Directive is valid for Austria, according to which physicians may be in service for a maximum of 48 hours a week. This normalization of working time will mean a considerable amount of burden to us, namely 300 million schilling per year. But we have to go in. These are the costs we have to live with if we do not want to reduce the medical standards. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! We liberals reject the current novel to the Medical Law, because we have some criticisms regarding this novel, and I would like to briefly list it to you. Point one: Learning practices: It is currently the case that small hospitals are given the opportunity to be training centres, provided that even subjects which do not include them may be taught by consiliar doctors working at these hospitals or by consiliar doctors who also conduct teaching practice, or if the trainee is allowed to do this in the teaching practice itself. This applies to subjects such as skin, psychiatry or in education as a doctor of general medicine for general medicine. We now demand that for all subjects that the doctors can do in their training outside the hospital, the employment relationship of the doctor remains. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] It can't be that an on-going general medicine doctor, because there's no general medicine in the hospital, can learn this subject in the hospital in an ambulance, because an ambulance is not a comparable training facility to a teaching practice. Point two: Group practice law. They know that the Constitutional Court rejected the ban on the operation of a group practice. You know that the abolition of this ban by 1. 4. In 1997, it entered into force. You know that the benefit-oriented health care fund with 1. 1. In 1997, provision must be made to ensure that patients who are to be transferred to the extramural area by means of service-oriented health care facilities are able to find structures suitable for inclusion. We are therefore in favour of the earliest introduction of the Group Practice Act – I will also make a corresponding amendment. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Point three: Doctoral Chamber Elections. As has been said, the election of doctors is to be combined for cost reasons. We think this is undemocratic, because this would lead to an extension of the electoral period, and because, as with the original vote in Vienna, which has recently taken place, a mere 56 percent acceptance of the medical board has occurred in a 50 % vote. With this little acceptance of the doctor's chamber, there would be an extension of the term of office of the officials in the doctor's chamber. We think this is undemocratic. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Point four – we have referred to this point four as the so-called "Lex Hamer": Under applicable law, foreign doctors in the border area may operate within the framework of consular activities, within the framework of scientific activities, within the framework of training activities for Austrian doctors. ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e5587	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Today we have two important legislative innovations to debate and decide what I would like to refer to in the first place on the legislation on medicinal products. This amendment aims at anchoring the EU Community authorisation procedure in Austrian law, which allows for more free movement of medicinal products in the European internal market. In the first place, two authorisation procedures must be distinguished: the decentralised and the central. The decentralisation is based on the principle of the recognition of a national authorisation granted by the first EU Member State by another Member State authority with a corresponding application for authorisation. However, each Member State shall be free if it objected to making such a claim in an appeal. It will not be rolled away from the national authorities. A single application for authorisation must be submitted directly to the European Medicines Agency in the central procedure, in particular in the case of genetic, high-tech products and medicinal products which represent a significant innovation. This draft law also deals with the equality of the pharmacovigilance of medicinal products in the European Economic Area, the demarcation and restriction of the concept of medicinal products and the use of radioactive substances. However, a very important point is the improvement of national and international pharmacovigilance. National pharmacovigilance systems must be established. Each pharmaceutical entrepreneur must constantly provide a qualified person responsible for pharmacovigilance. The Regulation covers undesirable medicinal products, drug abuse and quality defects. This is to be reported both nationally and internationally. These changes in the law are also necessary in terms of improvements and our accession to the EU. Among other things, the new Medicines Act fulfils the obligation to take account of the evolving EU legislation. The revision of the pharmaceutical EU directives requires a regulatory requirement to be complied with by the new Medicines Act. However, not only should formal matters be regulated here, but the high security system, which exists in Austria, should also be further developed and improved. While there is no doubt that state regulation in certain areas is either replaceable or ineligible, in this case almost no area can be overregulated when it comes to human health. This claim also gives rise to the regulatory priorities of the new law: the establishment of the future system for the authorisation of proprietary medicinal products, the reform of the rules within the meaning of the EU Pharmacovigilance System and the adaptation of the special Radiopharmacovigilance Scheme to the relevant EU provisions. Equally important – especially when you think about the incidents of recent times – are the focal points of the novelle on the Animal Disease Act, which leads to a reorganisation of the previous authorisation obligation, which guarantees better control in this area. In the field of radiopharmacovigilance, there is a special regulation of labelling, such as the explanation of the encodings used on the phyols in clear text or the amount of radioactivity per dose. The phyols themselves shall now include the name or code of the medicinal product, the operating control number and the expiry date, the international sign of radioactivity, the name of the manufacturer and the quantity of radioactivity. – So much for some innovations at the radiopharmaka. A new section on drug monitoring is to be added to the Medicines Act, according to which those who are entitled to wholesale and manufacture medicinal products have adverse drug reactions, often observed improper use and serious abuse and quality defects to report to the Ministry. In the event of such reports, the Federal Minister for Health will take steps to ensure the safety of medicinal products. The notification is now also subject to the authorisation of proprietary medicinal products, with detailed records of undesirable effects to be submitted to the Ministry after invitation or in certain periods of time after authorisation. These new provisions are intended to redesign the monitoring of medicinal products within the meaning of the EU. These changes are not primarily in terms of content, but above all in order to establish the formal aspects of the EU system for adverse drug effects in national law. In the revision of the Animal Disease Act, the adoption of certain provisions of two Directives on immunological veterinary medicinal products or pathogens of animal diseases is a priority. These are, on the one hand, the Directive laying down additional rules on immunological veterinary medicinal products. It shall allow Member States to carry out batch checks unless there is a release by the competent authority of another Member State. On the other hand, these are animal health and animal health conditions for trade in and import into the EU of products of animal origin. Member States shall ensure that trade in pathogens is subject to strict rules. The aim of this new pharmaceutical law is to lay down the rules for a regulation in this area within the European Union and to provide sufficient protection for consumers. The fact that the Federal Government does not incur any additional costs from these regulations is a desirable effect for all legislative projects. The lack of consideration for any costs has often been made a charge to the House. Similarly, there is no need for additional staffing due to the need for structural change in measures in view of the recognition of the Federal Institute for Animal Disease Control as a European testing institute for batch release of animal vaccines. All in all, Mrs. Minister, a successful company! In this sense, I wish the new law and you, Mrs Minister, best success in the interests of all of us.

In the Committee on Science, Public Health and Consumer Protection, you do so.]] Thank you for the flowers. [[Abg. May. Guggenberger: That's what we want!]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! To the Medicines Act or to the Medicines Act Novelle: I should like to remind you that the Medicines Act was adopted here in the House in 1982 and entered into force in 1983. The main objective of the Medicines Act was to create a well-founded legislative matter for the protection of public health. However, I should like to say that the way in which the proposal for a law was passed here in the course of the proposed Novelle Medicines Act is to be criticised, namely because the government template, the ministerial draft has been properly reviewed, but there have been substantial amendments which have not been submitted to the relevant interest groups and the experts involved. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! This too is a different access to a law. I say it again: The Medicines Act is an important, if not the most important, law on the protection of public health and I consider it a negligent way of dealing with the legislator or with the ministerial bureaucracy to pass such an important matter of law on the subject of assessment, at least as far as the amendments to the first draft are concerned. Ladies and gentlemen! With the Medicines Act 1983, a uniform drug concept was created – at the time the essentials of the law – a uniform term of medicine, which on the one hand includes an objective and on the other hand a subjective definition. What does that mean? A medicine is primarily a medicine if it develops medicinal product. This is the pharmacovigilance in the objective sense. But, of course, it would not be enough to include only those medicines in the Medicines Act which produce these objective medicinal products. Because then the possibility would be to remove dubious substances placed on the market as a pseudoar medicinal product from the entire scope of the Medicines Act by simply arguing that these substances do not achieve any medicinal effects at all. That was precisely the reason, ladies and gentlemen, that at the time the legislator introduced a subjective drug concept, which consists in the fact that those substances that do not produce any medicinal effects, but which are subjective to the concept of medicinal products, which the person who places them on the market, must be subject to. Ladies and gentlemen! Another serious point, which was then relevant to the Medicines Act in 1983, was a distinction between medicinal products and foodstuffs. This is the primary delimitation. And then there is a residual term, which is the so-called consumer products. In this sense, the Supreme Court of Justice, in competition proceedings, which have been tried but also the Administrative Court, in the authorisation procedure, has established the jurisdiction line that consumer products are part of the rest of the product alongside the medicinal products and foodstuffs. That is to say, this remaining-size Judicature has caused it, in conjunction with the proper interpretation of the law, to classify all substances depending on whether they are medicinal products, food or consumer products. Ladies and gentlemen! It is precisely this final assessment of the substances that I see as a danger by the government template which is now in place, namely because substances intended to be used in accordance with complementary medical methods are now excluded from the concept of medicinal products, provided that they do not serve to achieve medicinal effect after the subjective purpose of the seller or that which they place on the market, unless they are substances manufactured in accordance with homeopathic principles and techniques. That means, however, ladies and gentlemen, a break in the Judicature and the principle that once all substances are either medicinal products, food or consumer products – now another definition is added. And I can guarantee you one thing: namely, that this question, whether there is such a second remaining size or not, will be the reason to deal with the Supreme Court in competition proceedings or the Administrative Court in approval proceedings. Another criticism that I have here is the fact that, as I have already mentioned, these paramedical products are taken out of the scope of the Medicines Act, on the one hand, by the new point 9 introduced, and on the other hand, two pages are taken in again. One of my previous speakers, Mr Pumberger, has referred to this. On the one hand, they are excluded, they are not medicinal products, but on the other hand they are taken back in § 11c of the Medicines Act. In other words, those homeopathic or partially paramedical substances which are placed on the market as so-called proprietary medicinal products will be included again, subject to a separate authorisation procedure, which, however, consists in the fact that, if the Federal Ministry does not issue a ban, these substances are actually authorised. Mrs. Federal Minister! I can guarantee you one thing: that you will face a large number of requests, and I am eager to see how this bureaucratic effort will actually be dealt with. However, I suspect that this work, this pensum, cannot be handled at all, which, however, Mrs Federal Minister, results in the fact that these partially paramedical substances are then automatically authorised, because no prohibition decision has been taken. That's exactly where I see the danger. Dr. Pumberger rightly pointed out that manufacturers of such substances would naturally use the fact of non-prohibition by the Ministry as a sales argument when placing on the market. They will then note on the packaging, as mentioned above, such as: approved in accordance with § 11c of the Medicines Act, without any precise review procedure having taken place, because, as already mentioned, the authorisation applies automatically, unless such a prohibition decision would actually be adopted. Ladies and gentlemen! Let me briefly comment on the debate on the medical practitioners. The patient's own responsibility and self-responsibilities were rightly advised here. That's not a question at all. However, ladies and gentlemen, it has been overlooked that there are a large number of charlatans who take advantage of the often hopeless life situation of heavily ill people to do bad business with the hope. Ladies and gentlemen! That is exactly the decisive thing, and that is also the reason why the liberals are acting against the unlimited introduction of the medical profession in Austria. Do we, ladies and gentlemen, want to have German relations? Read the German illustrations, which reads: Do you want to become a medical practitioner? In six months you can become a medical practitioner. – These people are then let go of the patients who often have no other choice and see themselves shut down in their decision. Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, there are a variety of diseases – unfortunately God! – who cannot be cured, at best relieved, and of course the person who suffers from such a disease is inclined to seek other ways of treatment, and he is of course a welcome victim of these charlatans. That is why I think it is quite right that we should stick to this regulation and that the health practitioners in Austria should not let go of the patients. But this is not a value judgment or a qualification of alternative medicine, absolutely not: homeopathy is safe in the coming, that is no question at all. It is still a lot unexplored, and we do not know much about homeopathy yet, but it is nothing against it if the treatment takes place under the responsibility of a doctor. Ladies and gentlemen! No question at all: There are black sheep in every profession – a number of processes have already been suggested here, which are currently being taken from the courts. Everything you will never get under control, but remember that the medical profession in Austria is still the most prestigious profession. I do not want to talk here about where the political profession is in the people's reputation, in their own interest. But the medical profession is now a highly respected profession, and the people in Austria have great hopes, great expectation and great trust for the doctors, and this trust should not be destroyed.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e9958	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I would also like to comment briefly on the request for negotiation concerning measures to combat traffic noise in the railways. There is no doubt that traffic noise in general and rail noise is regarded as a burden in particular by the citizens who live on long-distance routes. We have already adopted a resolution here in the House in June 1993 asking the Federal Government to carry out intensive measures to combat traffic noise. Now I should like to remind you briefly of what was essentially stated in this motion for a resolution at the time. It is true that there was also a need for an amendment to the Railways Act, but it has also been called for to take the following measures: the adoption of a rail vehicle noise-admissibility regulation, a rail traffic noise-immission protection regulation and the fastest possible completion of the noise disaster. What has happened so far? – In concrete terms, ladies and gentlemen: A rail vehicle noise admissibility regulation was adopted in June 1993 setting the limit values for noise emissions caused by rail vehicles. A rail noise emission protection regulation was also adopted in June 1993 which contains rules for the necessary noise protection measures in the area of new construction and expansion routes. Furthermore, in the context of sound engineering renovation of the existing railway lines, emissions catastrophes have been created for the federal states, which have been in full existence since March 1994 and represent noise pollution along the existing railway tracks. With motion for a resolution adopted on 17 November. In June 1993, the Federal Minister for Public Economy and Transport was asked to open negotiations with the countries immediately in order to ensure the financing of measures against traffic noise. Unfortunately, these negotiations have not been successful everywhere – I would particularly like to address Mr Kukacka’s address – because in Upper Austria, the matter of how I am going to explain again later on in a concrete example. One thing has to be clear: over the past decades, noise problems along railway lines from all sides, i.e. also from the countries responsible for spatial planning and land use, have been given too little attention. As a result, the development was encouraged that land in the immediate vicinity of railway lines could de facto be fully built and without regard to noise emissions. It is precisely from this point of view that the financing of noise protection measures along existing railway lines is a common task of the federal, state and local authorities. In the context of a comprehensive treatment of noise protection on railway routes, negotiations are currently under way between the Federal Government and countries in various areas on sound engineering reorganisation of these routes, with the aim of agreeing on a financing model for the various measures taking into account the situation and, above all, the interest situation. These measures, of course, also include noise protection windows, not only noise protection walls and noise protection forests, but also in built-in areas. However, there are mainly problems in the distribution of costs. In essence, the federal government pays 50 percent, the rest of the municipalities that are active here, partly also the countries. Maybe some points to remind us of what it's all about: On the one hand, it is about the difference between high-performance lines and high-speed trains. The high-performance trains are about being able to drive up to 200 km per hour, to continue to use the conventional train and to enable a mixed transport route. The high-speed train is usually only about passenger traffic and about reaching a maximum speed of more than 250 km per hour. In 1989 Parliament passed the High Performance Tracking Act. It aims at the four-track extension of the Westbahn. Investments have already been made by 1995: 3.5 billion schilling for planning and construction. For the planning and construction of the west railway line between Vienna and Salzburg from 1996 to 2000, there is a frame of 14.5 billion Schilling. Ladies and gentlemen! This is a common EU project of a total construction volume of 175 billion schilling. It's a connection between Munich and Verona. With 1. January 1996 has begun the planning of the company, and at least the section in Austria will be reached from border Germany to border Italy in a timetable by 2010. ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e12669	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! In my speech, I would like to refer to two federal laws to be adopted, which are under discussion today, but which have not yet been discussed. With its entry into force, these laws will guarantee more safety in freight transport. The first thing to mention here is the Sea Shipping Act. This law incorporates some international conventions into the Austrian legal system. The reason for the accession to these Conventions was that some high seaships were flying under Austrian flag and thus certain obligations arose and arose. The crew of the Austrian naval fleet is currently about 400 men, who work on 28 vessels. These seaships are used exclusively in tramp shipping. In any event, some EU directives in the field of maritime law are to be transposed into Austrian law, for example the EU Council Directive 9475 of 22 December 1990 on the approximation of the laws of the Member States relating to maritime transport. Council Regulation (EC) No 2111/94 of 22 November 1994 laying down common rules for ship inspection and inspection organisations and the relevant measures of the maritime authorities. Under these directives, Austrian vessels must be built in accordance with the rules of a recognised classification society and maintained in such a way that they have the highest class of this classification society. The Maritime Compliance Act is therefore EU-compatible and therefore meets the increased requirements of EU maritime safety policy. Secondly, I would like to talk about the Federal Law on Safe Containers, which also stands for greater safety in transport. Austria has been a contracting partner of the International Convention on Safe Containers for several years. In addition to Austria, the Convention on Safe Containers includes more than 50 Member States. Almost all European States, including the States of the European Union, are parties to the Agreement on Safe Containers. This Convention provides that containers shall be subject to certain load tests before they are approved, marked and reviewed regularly. The law already regulates the necessary safety requirements for construction, approval, labelling, maintenance and control in the international container transport sector. The aim of this bill is to extend the application and enforcement of the international convention to the national field of multimodal means of transport. The present Container Safety Act will make a further significant contribution to increasing safety in national and international large container traffic, in particular by requiring the authorisation of containers in accordance with the provisions and equipment with a proper safety approval plate. This law also provides for the award of a test and identification number. In addition, the owner is obliged to maintain its container in safe condition and to undergo a check within 30 months at the latest five years of production. Implementing bodies shall be the bodies of the public security service and road surveillance, as well as the shipping police, the bodies of the transport labour inspectorate and the customs authorities. These bodies shall function for the District Administration Authority. The implementation of this federal law will, of course, also create personnel and material costs. However, these will be small, because there is no need for extensive training for control bodies. Nor is additional staff needed. The controls of containers can be carried out largely within the framework of other controls. If the costs of execution and the administrative charges are compared, for example, with those taken into account in the authorisation, these contributions will be compensated, and this will not burden the public budget. These provisions can significantly reduce safety risks, such as poor constructions or the defective condition of a container, which can occur in the event of a significant load on long distances, in particular when handling. This federal law will also provide positive impulses for the railway transport of large containers. The Austrian Federal Railways play a leading role in combined cargo transport in general as well as in large container transport in particular in Europe, ladies and gentlemen, since the beginnings of combined cargo transport. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] The focus of large container traffic is traditionally in the international field and here again analogous to the Austrian external trade structures in bilateral transport with Germany, but also with the Benelux countries as well as Italy, France and Switzerland. Transport quality on the rails will not least increase by this legal measure in favour of the market. As a result of this improvement in transport quality, there will also be a greater interest in freight rail transport with containers in the future. Moreover, the high economic strength and the increasing economic interrelationship between the regions in Europe are expected to increase above average in freight traffic. With the container transport, which is also called unaccompanied combined transport on the railway, the double amount of goods at the same trailer load can be transported as with conventional systems. However, we must also not forget the environmental impact, because more container transport on the rail means less on the road and less environmental damage, less pollutant emissions and less risks. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, it must be noted that it is certainly not possible to refuse to approve these two legislative texts – already in the Committee on Transport, one-way approval has been given – because, with the entry into force of these laws, not only specific risks of transport are reduced or even almost excluded, but also essential steps are taken with a view to a forward-looking transport policy.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e12964	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! As the last person who speaks about transport problems in today's debate, I agree to comment on a request made by Mr Parnigoni and Mr Magnella. Kukacka has already been introduced into the last Transport Committee. It concerns the creation of internationally binding strict exhaust rules for motor vehicles. Why this demand for internationally binding strict rules? – Because, as a small country, we simply know that we are not in a position to manage all of this alone in terms of the environmental impact of road transport and others. Let me make a few comments about this. Road transport – we all know – is one of the main causes of air pollution in addition to industry, energy generation in the case of caloric power plants, and house fire. Many pollutants are in the air today. I don't need to list them all. But especially in the case of nitrogen oxides, the transport of goods is the main cause, and only one tenth of the sulphur dioxide pollution comes from Austria. Nine-tenths are imported from neighbouring countries, but above all also by catless cars from our – most eastern neighboring countries. What forest damage is caused by traffic and air pollution? Austria has high ozone levels caused by car, and from 1980 to 1993 only a slight reduction could be achieved. As air pollutants and significant factors to weaken forests, hydrocarbons, sulphur dioxide, nitrogen oxides and their derivatives, such as ozone, have been identified. Car transport causes 66 percent of NOx emissions and 30 percent volatile organic compounds. Sulfur dioxide emissions could be drastically reduced in Austria. Nevertheless, the sulfate load by precipitation and the enrichment in the needles and leaves due to pollutant imports are still high. One thing we must also say: Not only the high ozone load in the summer, but above all the permanent load is problematic. Finally, a new study has been carried out by the Federal Environment Agency and the Seibersdorf Research Centre. The tolerable load values were exceeded in the forest, especially in alpine highs, up to eight times. And this, ladies and gentlemen, must cause us to think and force us to act. Since the introduction of the strict U.S. exhaust regulations for newly approved cars, which Austria made in 1986/87 as the first European country, Austria has become a pioneer in the field of pollutant limitation in the passenger cars. The EU itself did not achieve a similar development until 1992. Austria is also the first country – and we can point it out with pride – in which since 1. 1. 1993 with unbleached petrol. In 1996, a further halving of the current sum limit was decided in the EU, Euro II, and in a so-called multi-stage test. For the year 2000, a new limit was defined in autumn 1995, which is quite comparable to the American one. The sum of NOx and hydrocarbons is most likely to be only 0.2 grams per kilometre. The main objective of the EU is to reduce emissions from road transport by 60 to 70 percent by 2010. However, the introduction of the 3-way catalyst for petrol cars is not enough to achieve this. Lead-containing petrol should be completely prohibited by the year 2000 – including in our neighbouring countries – and EU uniform limit values, such as benzene and sulphur, should also be introduced for other fuels. Euro I and II resulted in considerable relaxation in NOx emissions, especially in heavy trucks. And there will also be a further reduction for 2000. What is the problem? – Every year, 100 000 tonnes of inferior foreign diesel fuels are also burned by domestic trucks. There are no figures on emissions in East-West transport or transit. Transport policy measures to limit ozone pollution require accurate detection of all pollutants imported from abroad, agreements with neighbouring countries on their reduction, and the introduction of strict exhaust gas standards for trucks and buses at our eastern neighbours. What measures can we take to reduce emissions, and we must also note that low speed limits, which are often called for, have proved to be ineffective? – State premiums for the scrapping of old cars without cat would be a possibility, in addition to the gas commuting lines already mentioned by colleagues Kopf. The Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection will be working on this shortly. Electric and solar cars, hybrid drive, hydrogen drive as possible future replacement of petrol drive cause even greater problems today. The new waste gas schemes for commercial vehicles, Euro III, recently adopted in the EU, are expected to bring, as early as 1997, a reduction in volatile emissions by 34 percent and by 84 percent by 2010, a reduction of NOx by 26 percent by 1997 and 67 percent by 2010. Finally, let me make one last comment: If we have to say today that many air pollutants are seriously affecting our environment and people's health, the answer to the question of where we should be directing freight, passenger transport, where we want it, on the road or on the rail, can clearly only be "the rail". It no longer surprises me when Mr Trenk comes out of the liberals, for example, and says: As long as the "F" are in Parliament, there is no consent to the Semmering Base Tunnel. – Mr Commissioner, I still assume that you do not know the dimensions on which it is concerned. It is not about shortening driving time, it is about the economic area Styria-Kärnten-Salzburg. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] It's about the trans-European network. It is about the international connections that we cannot conclude. And this is not chauvinistic, because as a border on the Styrian side I have been demanding and demanding it for years, but simply because we believe that we can and will need to build a work built around the turn of the millennium for the next hundreds of years. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-06-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00032_d7e14402	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! With the establishment of technical universities, a new education system at higher education level has been established in Austria, which is a necessary complement to the local education provision. Due to the relatively short vocational training of three years, the technical studies were created as an alternative to the science-theoretically oriented studies at the university. Not only do masters have the right to attend a vocational college, but they also have the right to teach and graduate from vocational schools. There are currently 20 vocational universities in Austria, and the interest in taking up the courses is very high. It is critical in the report of the University Council, as has been mentioned several times, that it is said that there is too little permeability. It is my particular concern, therefore, to take action and really do something to ensure that this offer can be used not only by masters but especially by people with completed teaching. This would be an image improvement, namely the image improvement we want for teaching. If a strenuous person thus had the opportunity to make a further education, then the teaching would not be a one-way road, but the practical basis for lifelong learning and higher qualification. For the future, the University of Applied Sciences Council sets the following objectives, which I personally consider very important: Quality and innovation of the courses and their regional relevance, examination of future needs in society and the economy – and this is important: we have to offer adequate training so that graduates can also receive a corresponding post –, integration of graduates into the economy and internationalisation of education offers. However, I also believe that a course of study on youth work and adult education would be very good. Today, youth work is almost exclusively concerned with youth welfare and drug therapy.
